## Consequential corrections

- Scan 003, upper-right oblique marginal note (s003-03): Restores the factor l₁^{-γ} in the conjugating element g₀.
- Scan 003, last display, small superscript on rho (s003-16): A live inverse on ρ differs from a cancelled inverse; the corrected relation cannot be settled from the expected algebra.
- Scan 005, two-line title (s005-02): The omitted title identifies the profinite loop group and its (0,3) index.
- Scan 005, Un endomorphisme de … (s005-05): No case choice is imposed; the witnesses disagree on the group symbol.
- Scan 005, interlinear insertion below the last sigma formula (s005-09): The deletion-layer reading remains provisional; the live inner-conjugation operand is retained as l_0^{-1}.
- Scan 007, class-normalizing phrase between two main prose lines (s007-06): The phrase specifies the exterior automorphism whose representative is being chosen.
- Scan 007, parenthetical description of L_0 (s007-08): The manuscript uses an isomorphism sign, not an equality sign.
- Scan 007, added character value above p in the multiplier sentence (s007-10): The addition identifies p with chi(u); it attaches before the membership statement.
- Scan 008, opening line, object of remplace (s008-02): Preserves a probable authorial g/u slip instead of silently changing the object of remplace.
- Scan 008, first display, argument of rho (s008-03): The visible l₀ is retained although the displayed expanded right side suggests that λ may have been intended.
- Scan 008, live replacement above far-right end of third system row (s008-08): Preserves the author’s unmatched parenthesis in the replacement expression.
- Scan 008, last live display, first int expression (s008-10): The unmatched closing parenthesis is preserved literally.
- Scan 008, last live display, int-rho expression before target l-infinity (s008-11): No balancing parenthesis is silently supplied.
- Scan 009, Annotations beneath u sigma_infty(l_0) (s009-07): Preserves the separate equality annotation and the scope of the lower bracket.
- Scan 009, Left underbrace in the second calculation (s009-09): Attaches l_0 to sigma_infty(l_1), excluding the preceding u.
- Scan 010, Marginal equations between abélien and donc (s010-07): The note asserts equality of beta and gamma and gives q=-r as its equivalent condition; it does not state the two separate identifications written by H.
- Scan 010, Last marginal formula, exponent on the final l_1 (s010-08): Both generator factors in the last marginal expression carry beta.
- Scan 010, Small insertion above que in the final paragraph (s010-17): The phrase supplies the additional condition p=±1 at the point en exigeant ... que.
- Scan 011, Group definition: name after equality (s011-16): The author names the group Γ.

### Remaining unresolved places

- opening, word between commutant and sigma-zero: \deleted{\unsure{à}} — Dense crossed knot; neither the underlying letter nor cancellation state can be fully distinguished.
- first display, argument of u: u(l_{\unsure{1|0}}) — The index is an opaque teardrop of ink. The target l₁^p suggests 1, but that is not a recovery of the individual strokes.
- long left marginal note: \unsure{\beta=\gamma|\beta\ne\gamma} — The comparison sign is oblique and crowded by adjacent letters.
- rewritten second relation, crossed exponent before beta: \deleted{\unsure{\gamma|\alpha}} — The crossed loop has no fully recoverable outline; omitted by both witnesses.
- last display, underlying first argument of int: \deleted{\unsure{h_1}} — Additional strokes under the live h₁′ are present, but every letter is not plain.

### Remaining unresolved places

- struck glyph in the title before profini: \deleted{\unsure{P|$\Pi$}} — The glyph is heavily retraced and crossed. Not all pre-cancellation strokes can be separated, and the boundary from the initial of profini may warrant review; retain the deletion doubt rather than resolving a struck letter from context.
- title hatted-group equality: \unsure{\hat\pi=\hat\pi_{0,3}|\hat\Pi=\hat\Pi_{0,3}} — Both signs have a long horizontal head and two nearly straight stems. Their size and strokes on this page do not reliably fix case.
- isolated left-margin sign: $\unsure{\partial|\delta}$ — The isolated closed oval with hooked oblique stem admits both signs; no local definition settles its function.
- endomorphisme de group symbol: \unsure{\pi_{0,3}|\Pi_{0,3}} — H and C disagree on the case. The low right stroke merges with the index region, so the crop does not separate the two forms confidently.
- struck operand inside the inserted int expression: \deleted{\unsure{l_0}} — Several dark strokes overlap the live operand. They do not yield a fully plain struck l and subscript 0. The deletion hypothesis is retained from C but requires root adjudication of the ink layering.
- first inserted symmetric-group name: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — The symbol is a rounded S with a hooked upper-right stroke and closed lower loop; this page alone does not define its typographic alphabet.
- final symmetric-group formula: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3^+|\mathcal S_3^+} — Same unresolved alphabet distinction as the inserted group name. The superscript plus and the isomorphism sign are plain.

### Remaining unresolved places

- Opening prose after pas de: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — The ornate handwritten S is clear, but the isolated page does not settle the LaTeX alphabet. This is an alphabet-classification uncertainty, not doubt about S or the index 3.
- Live dans at the right of the cancelled display: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — The ornate handwritten S is clear, but the isolated page does not settle the LaTeX alphabet. This is an alphabet-classification uncertainty, not doubt about S or the index 3.
- On a une représentation de: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — The ornate handwritten S is clear, but the isolated page does not settle the LaTeX alphabet. This is an alphabet-classification uncertainty, not doubt about S or the index 3.
- vu que ... opérant: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — The ornate handwritten S is clear, but the isolated page does not settle the LaTeX alphabet. This is an alphabet-classification uncertainty, not doubt about S or the index 3.
- Cancelled first display, middle product: \unsure{\sigma_0^{-1}\sigma_1^{-1}} — Cancellation hatching prevents every base letter/index from being verified; C’s proposed product remains possible.
- sur pi lui-bar-m insertion: \unsure{\pi|\Pi} — Thickened two-legged handwritten pi; case is not secure in the small inserted form.
- Main line after Z/3Z and its insertion: \unsure{provient|pvt} — The form has an unmistakable p-descender and final tall t but strongly compressed interior; a contraction pvt is possible. Both witnesses give provient. This is a newly marked certainty question, not an asserted expansion.
- Fixed-point insertion before the explanatory parenthesis: \unsure{\pm\bar j|-\bar j|\pm j} — Reinforcing strokes obscure the initial sign; the long horizontal above j may be a bar, although a very elongated dot is not wholly excluded.
- Cancelled word following qu’on: \deleted{\unsure{pourra}} — The cancellation covers the interior of the word; not all letters are plain.
- Short live form after the cancelled word, before next-line pris: \unsure{a} — The short form is probably a but has an unusually long initial descending stroke; preserved with a new doubt.
- Teichmüller insertion, group acted upon: \unsure{\pi_{0,3}|\Pi_{0,3}} — Both legs and indices are clear, but case cannot confidently be inferred from the small hand alone.
- Right of first calculation: \margin{$\unsure{2|z|t}$} — Newly noticed dark isolated mark; its status as a semantic inscription requires root adjudication.
- cancelled terminal factor in the third calculation: \deleted{\unsure{l_1}} — The cancellation tangles the lower index; the root reader does not resolve C’s struck uncertainty.

### Remaining unresolved places

- indexed group symbol in the opening sentence: \unsure{\pi_{0,3}|\Pi_{0,3}} — The horizontal head and two upright stems are clear; the second stem curves slightly at its foot. C gives pi and H gives Pi. Case cannot be securely inferred from graphic height alone. The index 0,3 remains as in both witnesses.
- first symmetric-group symbol: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — The rounded S-like form does not by itself settle the typographic alphabet; retain M0780.
- symmetric-group symbol in the product defining Gamma_0,3: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — Same local graphic form as the earlier symbol; the whole product formula was read in its crop, but the alphabet remains unresolved.
- crossed word before L_0: \deleted{\unsure{un}} — The heavy strike obscures the original low letter strokes. The reading un remains plausible but is not plain letter for letter.
- crossed word before live est le multiplicateur: \deleted{\unsure{est}} — A dense short cancellation precedes the legible live est. Individual original e/s/t strokes cannot all be separated confidently; C’s nested doubt is retained.
- small upper-line stroke following the inserted =chi(u): \inserted{$=\chi(u)$,} — Both witnesses read a comma. A comma-like stroke is visible just above the right endpoint of the curved insertion guide. Their near contact leaves a genuine placement/status question for root review; the proposed reading retains the comma within the upper-line addition.

### Remaining unresolved places

- Opening cancellation: \deleted{\unsure{pour $g$}} — The g descender is clear; several strokes of pour remain crossed and faint.
- Opening insertion, symbol after savoir: \unsure{\tau|\zeta} — The small symbol combines a short upper stroke with a hooked or zigzag continuation. Tau remains the first reading, but the local shape alone leaves a zeta-like alternative.
- Opening abbreviation: \unsure{correspond.|correspond.\textsuperscript{t}} — The stop is visible; a small raised adjoining trace is not securely separable from the nearby cancelled writing.
- Opening cancelled word before à: \deleted{\unsure{aux}} — Three low letter traces support aux, but the strike prevents certainty for every letter.
- NB cancelled word: \deleted{\unsure{deux}} — Initial d and terminal crossing are visible; the central e/u traces remain overlapped.
- NB function word ending: \unsure{fonction|fonctions} — The angular terminal tail can be a final n flourish or an additional s. The singular is retained first without grammatical normalization.
- Group definition, ornate group symbol: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — The ink establishes the ornate S with index 3, but not an exact typographic alphabet.
- Cancelled word following group definition: \deleted{\unsure{donnée}} — A leading ascending/rounded stroke and small humps support the proposed word; cancellation masks the interiors.
- Oblique margin relation: \unsure{=|\simeq} — The compact uneven strokes do not settle equality versus the proposed isomorphism sign.
- Oblique margin cancellation: \deleted{\unsure{groupe}} — The initial g-like descender and later rounded traces remain under a heavy strike; not every letter is plain.
- Oblique margin final group symbol: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — The ornate S is legible, while the exact font family remains uncertain.
- Closing cancellation: \deleted{\unsure{et}} — Dense diagonal strokes obscure much of the small loop and upright.

### Remaining unresolved places

- Cancelled relation, first product: u\unsure{g|\rho} — The head and hooked descender favour g; the cancelled strokes do not justify discarding rho. Root independently agreed with g first.
- Cancelled image formula, last index: L_{\unsure{1|0}} — The final index is densely blackened.
- Nested cancellation after déjà: \deleted{\unsure{$\operatorname{int}(g)$}} — The strike overlaps several of the proposed small letters.
- Last line of cancelled paragraph, first word: \unsure{par|près} — Overwriting prevents recovery of the whole word; retain the grammatical irregularity le donnée separately.
- Article before inserted sous-: \unsure{la|l'} — An a-shaped baseline body and an apostrophe-like upper stroke coexist. The correction sequence cannot be established.
- Ornamental S_3 in extension phrase: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — The ornamental letter does not securely distinguish modern font choices in this local instance.
- Cancelled abbreviation after first sigma-rho identity: \deleted{\unsure{p.ex.}} — The strike crosses the letters and stops after p.
- Cancelled noun below autom.: \deleted{\unsure{él\textsuperscript{t}}} — Individual upper and baseline strokes overlap the cancellation and replacement.
- Cancelled terminal baseline word/sign: \deleted{\unsure{de|$\sigma$}} — The short struck shape is not sufficiently resolved.
- Insertion at bottom edge: \inserted{\unsure{de carré $l_i$}} — The words are substantially supported, but the compressed final index at the edge remains uncertain.

### Remaining unresolved places

- Cancelled epsilon_0 definition, generator index: l_{\unsure{1|0}} — The diagonal strike touches the base/subscript area; not every index stroke is plain.
- Interlinear square relation after doit écrire: \varepsilon_{\unsure{\infty|0}}^2=l_\infty — The compact subscript has a small left component and closed right portion. It may be infinity or 0; the expected later formula does not settle the ink.
- Cancelled word after je: \deleted{\unsure{trouve}} — The horizontal crossing obscures the joined middle letters.
- Added word above the cancelled trouve: \inserted{\unsure{écris}} — The visible beginning supports écris, but the final small strokes meet the right image boundary.
- Lower grouping stroke beneath the first surviving square product: \unsure{\underbrace{\sigma_\infty l_\infty^\alpha l_0\sigma_\infty l_\infty^\alpha}} — The stroke extends to the last factor but has an internal upturn under the second sigma and a further curl; its grouping scope is not unambiguous.
- Second index in the added and struck (=) inverse expression: l_{\unsure{0|\infty}}^{-1} — The low outline has a broad curved form which can be read as one 0 with the preceding l foot or as a compact infinity. The expected inverse identity is not used to decide it.
- Doubly cancelled prefix after live i.e.: \deleted{\unsure{l_\infty^{-1}l_\infty}} — Both subscripts are crossed by a thick horizontal line. A downward trace supports 1 for the second, but full underlying forms remain obscured.
- Word of the right marginal query: \unsure{prends} — The low middle and dark looped ending support prends provisionally but do not distinguish every terminal letter.
- Overwritten value in the right marginal query: \alpha=\unsure{2|1} — Overlapping upright, curved entry and lower foot do not separate a unique surviving digit.

### Remaining unresolved places

- Deleted word after σ_i in first prose line: \deleted{\unsure{par}} — The p descender is plain, but the horizontal strike obscures the middle and terminal letters. This is a new certainty qualification, not a substantive change from C’s proposed word.
- Deleted word after rho in On retrouve line: \deleted{\unsure{comme}} — The open initial c and remaining low traces support comme; several letters merge with the strike. This new doubt preserves the deletion and C’s proposed word.
- Last epsilon in cancelled continuation: \unsure{\varepsilon_\infty|\varepsilon_1} — The last lower trace is compact and not fully resolvable; no expected factor is supplied from the calculation.
- Deleted superscript in marginal derivation: \deleted{\unsure{2}} — The bent numeral-like trace is obscured by the cancellation.
- Final word at lower edge: \unsure{élém\textsuperscript{ts}} — The abbreviation is supported, but the small raised ending does not securely distinguish plural ts from singular t.

### Remaining unresolved places

- Opening insertion: cancelled stable: \deleted{\unsure{stable}} — Dark transverse cancellation masks several small letter strokes; not every letter is plain.
- Expanded product: first factor: \unsure{l_1|l_1'} — A detached short oblique tick lies above/right of the initial factor. Its status as a prime is not decisive; the unprimed C reading remains first.
- Upper cancelled first-underbrace trial: first index: l_{\unsure{1}}\sigma_\infty — The first l is traversed by the local cancellation; a dark lower stroke favors 1 but is partly merged with the crossing.
- First live epsilon identity: last subscript: \varepsilon_{\unsure{1}}^{-1} — The final subscript is heavily inked, with a vertical foot compatible with 1. Its upper portion is a dark blot and cannot be recovered more exactly.
- Final line: cancelled word: \deleted{\unsure{utiliser}} — The u/t/i/l/i sequence is visible; the later connected letters are traversed by the dark strike. The first reading utiliser is retained with local uncertainty.

### Remaining unresolved places

- first display, right Pi index: \unsure{1|*} — Overwritten, branching dark strokes do not isolate either complete alternative.
- cancelled word after P, barred P: \deleted{\unsure{qui}} — A q-like loop survives, but the remaining letters and i dot are not independently clear.
- cancelled baseline verb before induisent: \deleted{\unsure{échangent}} — Beginning and g descender support the word; the cancellation covers several central and final strokes.
- inserted and then struck replacement above échangent: \inserted{\deleted{\unsure{fixent}}} — New local doubt: f, the i dot, x and the terminal t-like stroke are visible, but e/n are flattened by the horizontal strike. Agreement of C and H does not resolve them.
- cancelled word before live sur: \deleted{\unsure{resp|sur}.} — Right descender and stop favor resp.; the obscured body does not exclude C’s sur. The terminal point is certain.
- cancelled rotations below symétries: \deleted{\unsure{rotations}} — New local doubt: opening r/o, tall strokes and the terminal ons contour are recognizable, but the heavy cancellation flattens the middle a/ti sequence.
- cancelled axis notation before (0,2): \deleted{$\unsure{P,\bar P}$} — Hatching obscures the letter heads and whether the bar is independent of the cancellation.
- p.r. au/un plan: \unsure{au|un} — au is favored, but the compressed first body and shallow following strokes do not conclusively distinguish the alternatives.
- first On considère, ornate S3: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — Ornate S and index 3 are clear; the exact TeX alphabet remains a representational uncertainty.
- cet ordre, et ornate S3 times Z/2: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — Rounded outer sweep and returning inner curve do not settle the supplied typographic alternatives.
- membership following the later dotted sigma list: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — The S identity and 3 are clear; the exact alphabet remains unresolved.
- raised and struck extension label: \inserted{$\deleted{\unsure{\mathcal E_0}}$} — Crossed and overwritten E-like strokes with a low closed index support E0. The exact index and stroke sequence remain uncertain, although insertion and cancellation are recorded.
- product beginning the last prose line: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — Same local ornate S form; neither supplied alphabet is decisively distinguished.
- upper U subscript in final display: \deleted{\unsure{0,3}} — The cancellation crosses both small digit bodies; the lower replacement U0,3 is clear but does not license resolving the old subscript.
- product under the final display brace: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — Identity S3 clear; typographic alphabet left open.

### Remaining unresolved places

- Initial cancelled word after Malgoire: \deleted{\unsure{correspondant|comprenant}} — Middle letters are crossed; the late descender favours the first reading but does not establish every letter.
- Initial tuple second component: \unsure{l|\lambda} — Tall upper loop favours l; anomalous left branch and retracing permit lambda.
- First fibre subscript in the inline map: U'_{\unsure{a|x}} — Oval with right downstroke favours a; the small retraced shape does not safely exclude x.
- Within cancelled stable phrase, between par and la: \unsure{à} — Separate low loop and upper accent-like stroke are real, but cancellation prevents full recovery.
- p.r. ... plan: \unsure{au|à} — Upper accent visible; terminal continuation may be u or an exit stroke.
- Before passant: \unsure{ne|un} — Compressed pair favours ne but its individual letters are not fully separable.
- Cancelled word before Désignant: \deleted{\unsure{Au}} — A-like initial and u-like ending remain crossed and entangled.
- Destination list after en allant vers: \unsure{a_0,a_1,a_\infty|0,1,\infty} — All three forms are retraced with a-like loops; the separate before/after states cannot be securely recovered.
- Middle factor in relation (1): \unsure{l'_1|l_1} — A separate high tick can be a prime, but an incidental mark remains possible.
- Between ! and On peut l’écrire: \unsure{?} — Loop and descending/forked stroke do not form a fully plain ordinary question mark.

### Remaining unresolved places

- Upper display, first cancelled old product: \unsure{l_0\rho} — The loops and lower index are traversed by heavy local strokes.
- Upper display, second cancelled old product: \unsure{l_1\rho} — The narrow proposed index and rho body are blackened by the cancellation.
- First marginal trial, trailing sigma: \unsure{\sigma_\infty} — The sigma body and proposed infinity are overlaid by dense hatching.
- First marginal trial, parenthetic product: \unsure{l_0\sigma_0\sigma_0\rho^{-1}} — The sequence has crossed loops, with the internal indices and final inverse insufficiently separate.
- Second marginal trial, trailing sigma: \unsure{\sigma_1} — A sigma-like top is visible but its index is obscured by local crossing strokes.
- Third marginal trial, trailing sigma: \unsure{\sigma_\infty} — The trailing body and subscript are crossed; every component cannot be certified.
- Small side trial beside second marginal row, left member: \unsure{l_0} — The small looped l and lower round trace are crowded into the side of the row.
- Small side trial beside second marginal row, right member: \unsure{\rho^{-1}\sigma_0} — The compact superscript and final index are entangled with crossing strokes.
- Cancelled lower-left trace beside the live marginal identity: \unsure{\sigma_\infty} — A sigma-like body and possible infinity remain crossed. Its lower-left position is visible but an underbrace is not.
- Third cancelled marginal action, right member: \unsure{l_1^{-1}l_0^{-1}} — The product is small and struck; the exponent strokes cannot all be separated from the cancellation.
- Large cancelled argument, puisque: \unsure{puisque} — The compact word is compatible with puisque, but its middle is crossed and not every letter is plain.
- Lower cancelled word: \unsure{distincts|abstrait\textsuperscript{t}} — The opening loop and tall stroke favor d and the short terminal sequence favors distincts; the horizontal strike still masks medial letters.
- Cancelled lower-left marginal sigma trace, attachment: \deleted{\unsure{\sigma_\infty}} — The trace is lower-left of the live second equality; no actual underbrace fixes its attachment.
- Group letter after présentation de in the large cancellation: \unsure{\Pi|\pi} — Tall two-legged outline favors Pi, but the curved right foot leaves case locally uncertain.
- Short horizontal marks surrounding la relation: \begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=(relation.base)]
\node[inner sep=0pt,outer sep=0pt] (relation) {la relation};
\draw ([xshift=0.05em,yshift=0.35ex]relation.north west) -- ++(0.42em,0);
\draw ([xshift=0.77em,yshift=0.35ex]relation.north west) -- ++(0.42em,0);
\draw ([xshift=1.49em,yshift=0.35ex]relation.north west) -- ++(0.42em,0);
\draw ([xshift=2.21em,yshift=0.35ex]relation.north west) -- ++(0.42em,0);
\draw ([xshift=2.93em,yshift=0.35ex]relation.north west) -- ++(0.42em,0);
\draw ([xshift=0.40em,yshift=-0.35ex]relation.south west) -- ++(0.42em,0);
\draw ([xshift=1.12em,yshift=-0.35ex]relation.south west) -- ++(0.42em,0);
\draw ([xshift=1.84em,yshift=-0.35ex]relation.south west) -- ++(0.42em,0);
\draw ([xshift=2.56em,yshift=-0.35ex]relation.south west) -- ++(0.42em,0);
\end{tikzpicture} — Five short upper strokes and four lower strokes are preserved geometrically; their function is not readable from the local arrangement.
- Live et sigma, exact attachment: \inserted{et $\sigma$} — The text is readable and live; its elevated position does not uniquely specify the scope of replacement.
- M0826: \unsure{1|0} — The second index is narrow and bent, favoring 1, but less distinctly formed than the first.
- M0827: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — The large ornate S has a rounded outer sweep and hooked upper end; this local glyph does not securely distinguish the two typographic alphabets.
- M0828: \unsure{comme on voit} — The connected first slanted line permits the offered words, but several joins do not separate into certain letters.
- M0829: \unsure{ils sont} — The short middle line is compressed and partly adjacent to the display; the individual letters remain uncertain.
- M0830: \unsure{\uline{pas} conjugués} — Pas has its own short independent underline. The following word remains crowded; a terminal horizontal stroke may belong to its ending or to a second short underline.
- M0831: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — The final ornate S repeats the broad rounded and hooked outline, without a decisive local alphabet distinction.
- M0832: \unsure{on a le groupe $(\sigma_i)$} — The opening short words and looped group-word outline are plausible; the final parenthesized symbols overlap other writing and remain uncertain.
- Small margin, short stroke near the end of conjugués: \unsure{\uline{pas} conjugués} — The line under pas is distinct. A later short horizontal stroke may be a second line, but its independence and scope are not secure; it is not extended across the absent intervening space.

### Remaining unresolved places

- (8), row 1 first l0 exponent: \deleted{\unsure{-1}} — Small crossed superscript; neither the sign nor digit can be fully isolated through the cancellation.
- (8), row 1 final l exponent: \deleted{\unsure{-1}} — Small crossed superscript; neither the sign nor digit can be fully isolated through the cancellation.
- (8), row 2 first l exponent: \deleted{\unsure{-1}} — Small crossed superscript; neither the sign nor digit can be fully isolated through the cancellation.
- (8), row 2 final l-infinity exponent: \deleted{\unsure{-1}} — Small crossed superscript; neither the sign nor digit can be fully isolated through the cancellation.
- (8), row 3 final l exponent: \deleted{\unsure{-1}} — Small crossed superscript; neither the sign nor digit can be fully isolated through the cancellation.
- (8), row 1 final l subscript: \unsure{1} — New doubt: the subscript combines a dark upright with looped overwritten remains; the current numeral cannot be confidently separated.
- (8), row 2 first l subscript: \unsure{1} — New doubt: a heavy vertical/foot-like trace overlaps low loops, so agreement of the drafts does not securely establish the index.
- (8), row 3 final l subscript: \unsure{0} — New doubt: a dark enclosing oval is visible but has doubled internal openings and overlapping strokes; current 0 is favored, not resolved.
- square verification, l immediately after underbrace: \unsure{1} — The small index is heavily blackened and retains overlapping upright and looping strokes.
- NB paragraph, cancelled baseline phrase: \deleted{\unsure{il faudrait}} — Crossing line and raised q descender obscure the original phrase; not every letter is plain.
- NB paragraph, cancelled lower-line word: \deleted{\unsure{ici}} — Short crossed upright and curved traces remain compatible with ici without securing all three letters.
- relations block, cancelled factor after rho: \deleted{\unsure{l_\infty}} — The factor is crowded against the opening parenthesis and heavily crossed.
- relations block, first parenthesized placeholder: \unsure{..|\ldots} — Two marks can be seen to the right of the cancellation; an additional left mark might be hidden. The later placeholders are independently visible and do not determine this count.
- (9), row 1 extra cancelled glyph: \deleted{\unsure{\varepsilon}} — The separate curved glyph is crossed and retraced; its epsilon-like outline is not completely plain.
- final word: \unsure{définition} — The last part is too compressed to recover each terminal letter; no expansion or grammatical normalization is made.
- earlier overwritten layers around rho and the live l factors in the derivation and (9): l_0;\quad l_0,l_1,l_\infty — Material-state uncertainty only: the live factors are readable and their C marks are resolved. Extra lower loops, vertical remnants and small slashes, particularly below the final infinity of (9), do not identify a separate earlier factor or index securely enough to assign letters. They are documented without inventing an additional cancelled factor.

### Remaining unresolved places

- Left margin, cancelled product: \deleted{$\unsure{[\rho^{-1}\sigma_0\rho\sigma_0\rho^{-1}][\underbrace{\rho\sigma_0\rho^{-1}}_{\sigma_1}\underbrace{\rho^{-1}\sigma_0}_{\sigma_\infty}\rho^{-1}]|[\rho^{-1}\sigma_0\rho\sigma_0\rho^{-1}][\underbrace{\rho\sigma_0\rho^{-1}}_{\sigma_1}\underbrace{\rho^{-1}\sigma_0\rho}_{\sigma_\infty}\rho^{-1}]}$} — Diagonal hatching obscures factor boundaries and the sigma_infinity underbrace endpoint. The ending =1 is excluded by recovered blank space beyond the close bracket.
- Left margin, unreadable crossed item between the two sigma identities: \deleted{\illegible{1}} — A short word-like crossed group has no securely recoverable full letter sequence. It is preserved as one illegible item.
- Left margin, cancelled first expression for l_0: \deleted{\unsure{(\rho\sigma_0\rho^{-1})^2}} — Crossing strokes obscure interior letters and the power; the complete expression remains doubtful.
- Left margin, cancelled first rho in l_1: \deleted{\unsure{\rho}} — Loop/descender remnants favor rho, but the body is covered by deletion.
- Left margin, initial-rho exponent in l_infinity: \unsure{\rho^{\deleted{-1}}|\rho^{-1}} — A thick zigzag favors cancellation of -1, but a live inverse remains a possible ink state. The algebraic prompt did not settle the reading.
- Left margin, cancelled terminal rho in l_infinity: \deleted{\unsure{\rho}} — A rho-like trace remains under hatching; not every stroke is distinguishable.
- Right NB, crossed final term: \deleted{\unsure{l_\infty^{-1}}} — Index shape survives better than letter and exponent; whole term stays doubtful.
- Main NB, cancelled donc: \deleted{\unsure{donc}} — The d/o survive but the n/c overlap the heavy strike. This new doubt does not count as a substantive change to C.
- Main NB, abbreviation after un mot: \unsure{p.v.|p.r.} — The second letter is v-shaped, but a compressed r cannot be excluded.
- Compatibility paragraph, first cancelled clause: \unsure{si dans ces dernières on avait tout pris des primes} — The two-line cancellation intersects several letter bodies; no whole-clause certainty is claimed.
- Compatibility paragraph, cancelled qu’on interprète: \unsure{qu'on interprète} — The first reading is favored, but the strike still crosses part of the q/on and subsequent letter run.
- Compatibility paragraph, cancelled sigma index: \unsure{\dot\rho(i)} — The overdot is visible but the cancelled index body is partly entangled.
- Cancelled phrase below i.e.: \unsure{de notre groupe} — The wavering strike crosses low portions of multiple letters.
- Cancelled final group symbol: \unsure{\mathcal E'|\sigma_i'} — The body looks E-like and the prime is clear, but the cancellation could create an E middle stroke and obscure a sigma subscript.
- Relation (7), overwritten index before k: \deleted{\unsure{j}} — A long descender is present; the head cannot be separated from retracing.
- Relation (7), cancelled comment word: \unsure{\text{dernier}|\text{devenir}} — The internal r/n or v/n sequence is crossed; initial d alone does not settle it.
- Relation (8), first cancelled exponent: \deleted{\unsure{-1}} — Dense local cancellation prevents a fully plain minus and 1.
- Relation (8), last index in epsilon_0 row: \unsure{0|1} — The closed oval favors 0 but retracing leaves 1 as an alternative.
- Relation (8), second cancelled exponent: \deleted{\unsure{-1}} — The raised signs are substantially overwritten.
- Relation (8), last exponent in epsilon_infinity row: \unsure{-1} — The horizontal/curving part overlaps a large strike; retain provisional -1.

### Remaining unresolved places

- M0843: \overset{\deleted{\unsure{\sim}}}{\unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3}} — The ornate alphabet remains unresolved. A separate heavily cancelled upper mark is present; its underlying tilde is retained as doubtful.
- M0844: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\tilde{\mathfrak S}_3|\mathcal S_3|\tilde{\mathcal S}_3} — The upper loop of S and nearby groupe descender prevent a secure independent-tilde reading. Both alphabet and adornment alternatives are retained, preferring the unadorned local outline.
- M0845: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\tilde{\mathfrak S}_3|\mathcal S_3|\tilde{\mathcal S}_3} — The margin has an ornate S3, but neighboring strokes and the letter's own loop do not isolate a definite tilde.
- M0846: \unsure{(} — Curved strokes before en overlap the slanted margin; a distinct opening parenthesis cannot be separated with certainty.
- Cancelled parenthesis in (13): \unsure{=\rho} — The equals-like strokes and descending rho are partly recoverable, but the scribble crosses the upper letter body and bar region.
- Cancelled point-name before live i: \unsure{a_i} — The old short point-name is blackened; neither the full a nor its proposed lower i can be certified through the cancellation.
- First ornate S, identity of the cancelled upper mark: \deleted{\unsure{\sim}} — The separate mark is certainly cancelled, but its underlying complete shape is not exposed.
- Main loop-image sentence, first right member after l-infinity: l_{\unsure{1|0}}^{-1} — A narrow dark upright overlies a small loop. One is preferred, but the old rounded component prevents full exclusion of zero.
- Introduction to (15), word after à: \unsure{sv|savoir} — Only a highly compressed returning stroke and short upper run are clear; a spelled-out word and an abbreviation cannot be distinguished securely.

### Remaining unresolved places

- M0847: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — Gamma identity in prose: the large sign has a broad outer loop, an upper returning stroke and an inner curling lower stroke, with a distinct subscript 3. It is consistently ornate S-shaped here, but its ductus alone does not determine a modern fraktur versus calligraphic alphabet.
- M0848: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — (11), source of phi: the large sign has a broad outer loop, an upper returning stroke and an inner curling lower stroke, with a distinct subscript 3. It is consistently ornate S-shaped here, but its ductus alone does not determine a modern fraktur versus calligraphic alphabet.
- M0849: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — (11), factor after Gamma=: the large sign has a broad outer loop, an upper returning stroke and an inner curling lower stroke, with a distinct subscript 3. It is consistently ornate S-shaped here, but its ductus alone does not determine a modern fraktur versus calligraphic alphabet.
- M0850: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — (12), source of the group-to-E arrow: the large sign has a broad outer loop, an upper returning stroke and an inner curling lower stroke, with a distinct subscript 3. It is consistently ornate S-shaped here, but its ductus alone does not determine a modern fraktur versus calligraphic alphabet.

- (10), first product, cancelled rho before the last factor: \deleted{\unsure{\rho}} — The long descender remains visible; the head is covered by several cancellation strokes. The rho reading is plausible but not fully legible.
- (10), second product, cancelled rho before the last factor: \deleted{\unsure{\rho}} — The long descender remains visible; the head is covered by several cancellation strokes. The rho reading is plausible but not fully legible.
- (10), third product, cancelled rho before the last factor: \deleted{\unsure{\rho}} — The long descender remains visible; the head is covered by several cancellation strokes. The rho reading is plausible but not fully legible.

### Remaining unresolved places

- opening line, image ... de Z/2: \unsure{inverse|réciproque} — Dotted initial and low sequence favor inverse; no p/q descenders are identifiable, but the compressed middle is not fully separable.
- cancelled word before avec: \deleted{\unsure{relati.}} — The cancellation covers the middle letters. A full relation is not visible.
- (17), first row, first overwritten right-hand index: \deleted{\unsure{0}} — The live infinity covers the old index.
- (17), first row, second overwritten right-hand index: \deleted{\unsure{\infty}} — The dark live 1 and crossing strokes obscure part of the old form.
- (17), second row, first overwritten right-hand index: \deleted{\unsure{\infty}} — The new dark zero overlies most of the old form.
- lower paragraph, ornate S with subscript 3: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — Letter and subscript are clear, but the handwritten ornament does not decide the typographic alphabet.
- lower paragraph, cancelled extérieurs: \deleted{\unsure{extérieurs}} — The initial e/x/t and accented é, plus the final s-like curve, favor extérieurs; the horizontal cancellation merges several compressed internal strokes. H also marks this reading uncertain.
- (18), small vertical sign below double-hatted Gamma: \unsure{\Vert|\cup} — The left descending stroke curls right at its foot and almost joins the shorter right stroke; the ink does not securely separate two vertical bars from a cup-shaped sign.

### Remaining unresolved places

- Opening commutant group sign: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — Ornate S-shaped ink and the subscript3 are present; the precise font alphabet cannot be inferred from mathematical meaning.
- Second intersection formula, ornate group sign: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — Ornate S-shaped ink and the subscript3 are present; the precise font alphabet cannot be inferred from mathematical meaning.
- Upper marginal product, ornate group sign: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — Ornate S-shaped ink and the subscript3 are present; the precise font alphabet cannot be inferred from mathematical meaning.
- Lower marginal product, ornate group sign: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — Ornate S-shaped ink and the subscript3 are present; the precise font alphabet cannot be inferred from mathematical meaning.
- Opening insertion, words: \unsure{qui contient} — Contient is favored over the discarded centralise alternative, but the compressed last letters remain insufficiently separate.
- First intersection, overwritten ornate letter: \deleted{\unsure{\mathfrak S_3}} — The replacement Gamma and low dark strokes obscure part of the prior symbol and its index.
- First intersection, proposed subscript: \unsure{0,3} — The low oval and angular strokes overlap the prior letter, so0,comma,3 are not individually certified.
- First intersection, complete right replacement term: \unsure{\Gamma_{\unsure{0,3}}|\mathbb\Gamma} — Thick retracing can resemble a doubled left wall; the lower cluster may be a subscript or part of the overwritten material.
- Separate cancelled NB: \deleted{\uline{\unsure{NB}}} — Letter outlines remain under dense diagonal cancellation; retain the word doubt.
- Hatted map letter in L_0 definition: \unsure{\hat k_0|\hat\kappa_0} — The long ascender and two arms favor k, but the local hand does not decisively exclude kappa; the circumflex and0 index are clear.
- g_i in (21): \unsure{\deleted{\unsure{-1}}|\unsure{-1}} — Cancellation is the preferred state because of extra angular/upward strokes. The underlying minus and1 remain occluded, and a live inverse cannot be excluded from ink alone.
- g_infinity in (22): \unsure{\deleted{\unsure{-1}}|\unsure{-1}} — Cancellation is the preferred state because of extra angular/upward strokes. The underlying minus and1 remain occluded, and a live inverse cannot be excluded from ink alone.
- g_1 in (22): \unsure{\deleted{\unsure{-1}}|\unsure{-1}} — Cancellation is the preferred state because of extra angular/upward strokes. The underlying minus and1 remain occluded, and a live inverse cannot be excluded from ink alone.
- g_0 in (22): \unsure{\deleted{\unsure{-1}}|\unsure{-1}} — Cancellation is the preferred state because of extra angular/upward strokes. The underlying minus and1 remain occluded, and a live inverse cannot be excluded from ink alone.

### Remaining unresolved places

- Opening ornate S alphabet: \unsure{\mathfrak S|\mathcal S} — The pen form is ornate and looped; assigning a typographical alphabet would go beyond the distinguishable ink.
- Opening S subscript left delimiter: \unsure{[|\{} — The short downstroke and foot favor a bracket, but its top is partly entangled with the S flourish. The right delimiter is clearly ].
- Underbrace S3 alphabet: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — The small ornamental S form is compatible with both alphabet labels.
- Cancelled phrase after conjugaison: \deleted{\unsure{au dessus des}} — The strike and a descender cross the central and final letters; the whole phrase therefore remains doubtful.
- Cancelled sign before first invariant-list tau: \deleted{\unsure{\dot\rho}} — The apparent rho and its possible dot cannot be completely separated from the over-strokes.
- Inserted sont after live rho: \unsure{sont} — Compressed middle strokes and a large final crossing prevent a fully certain lexical reading.
- Inserted savoir phi(S3): S alphabet: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — The pen form does not decide the alphabet; insertion placement and numeral are clear.
- De phi(S3): S alphabet: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — The broad ornamental S cannot settle the conventional alphabet name.
- Pi in Pi-conjugués: \unsure{\Pi|\pi} — The cap and two legs favor capital Pi, but the retraced lower strokes leave case locally uncertain.
- Cancelled conj: \deleted{\unsure{conj}} — The inner strokes remain covered; no final abbreviation stop is independently visible.
- Cancelled qui after E: \deleted{\unsure{qui}} — The q-like loop and descender are suggestive, but the following strokes are entangled.
- Live soit/sont after qui: \unsure{soit|sont} — The middle upright/connection is retraced and does not decide i versus n.
- Cancelled sigma0,tau formula after prendre: \deleted{$\unsure{\sigma_0,\tau}$} — Both symbols and especially the first index are crossed; uncertainty is retained over the complete formula.
- Les before produits: final-letter surface state: \unsure{les} — Only one word is visible. The dense final s does not separate retracing from a local rewrite; it does not support an additional preceding cancelled token.
- Cancelled tau before rho: \deleted{\unsure{$\tau$}} — The underlying bar and turn overlap the cancellation.
- Cancelled terminal s after Le: Le\deleted{\unsure{s}} — The cancellation occupies the terminal-s position, but the original small contour is entangled.
- Cancelled s after pt: pt\deleted{\unsure{s}} — The live p and t are clear; the crossed final small letter is not fully recoverable.
